# Funding Proposal

## Project Title: Climate Resilient Agriculture in Somalia (Ugbaad)

## Country(ies): Somalia

## Accredited Entity: FAO

## Date of first submission: 2024/05/12

## Date of current submission: 2024/06/09

## Version number: V.06

---

### Funding Proposal

The Green Climate Fund (GCF) is pleased to announce the funding proposal for the project titled "Climate Resilient Agriculture in Somalia (Ugbaad)." This project aims to enhance the resilience of agricultural systems in Somalia, particularly in the Ugbaad region, to mitigate the impacts of climate change.

### Project Overview

The proposed project will focus on the following key areas:

1. **Climate Change Impact Assessment**: Conduct a comprehensive assessment of the current and projected climate change impacts on Somalia's agricultural sector, with a particular emphasis on the Ugbaad region.
2. **Adaptation Measures**: Develop and implement adaptation measures to enhance the resilience of agricultural systems in the face of climate change. This includes the promotion of sustainable farming practices, such as agroforestry and conservation agriculture, as well as the development of early warning systems for extreme weather events.
3. **Capacity Building**: Provide training and capacity building programs for farmers, agricultural extension workers, and other stakeholders to enhance their knowledge and skills in climate-resilient agriculture practices.
4. **Policy and Institutional Support**: Advocate for the development of policies and institutional frameworks that support climate-resilient agriculture in Somalia, including the establishment of national climate change adaptation strategies and the integration of climate considerations into national development plans.
5. **Monitoring and Evaluation**: Establish a monitoring and evaluation framework to track progress towards the project's objectives and ensure accountability for the use of funds.

### Budget

The proposed budget for this project is as follows:

- **Personnel Costs**: $1,200,000 (including salaries, benefits, and training)
- **Material Costs**: $500,000 (including equipment, supplies, and materials)
- **Travel and Logistics**: $300,000
- **Contingency Fund**: $200,000

### Conclusion

The Green Climate Fund is committed to supporting the implementation of this project in Somalia. We believe that by enhancing the resilience of agricultural systems in the face of climate change, we can help ensure food security and sustainable development for all Somali communities.

---

### Section E: Logical Framework
- A logical framework outlining the relationships between inputs, activities, outputs, and outcomes

### Section F: Risk Assessment and Management
- An assessment of potential risks and a plan to mitigate them

### Section G: GCF Policies and Standards

### Section H: Annexes
- Additional information, such as supporting documents or references

Accredited Entities should provide summary information in the proposal with cross-references to annexes such as tables of contents and appendices. 43. Effective governance is also hindered by uncertain land tenure rights and land ownership, frequent disputes, and the absence of a widely recognized and enforceable legal system for land registration. There is also due to the weakness of the state, a lack of environmental regulations.
43. Due to the clan-based social structure in Somalia, land use planning must consider local dynamics and conflicts, including those between different ethnic groups, tribes, and religions. However, there is often limited knowledge and awareness among the local population about their rights and responsibilities under the law, which can lead to disputes and conflicts over land use.
43. In addition, the lack of a clear legal framework for land use planning and management can create uncertainty and confusion among landowners, tenants, and other stakeholders. This can result in disputes and conflicts over land use, as well as delays or failures to obtain necessary permits or approvals.
43. To address these challenges, it is important to strengthen the legal framework for land use planning and management in Somalia. This can include developing clear and consistent laws and regulations that protect the rights of all stakeholders, providing education and training on land use planning and management, and establishing mechanisms for resolving disputes and conflicts over land use.
43. In addition to strengthening the legal framework, it is also important to promote transparency and accountability in land use planning and management. This can include making land use plans and other relevant documents publicly available, providing opportunities for public participation and input, and establishing mechanisms for monitoring and evaluating the effectiveness of land use planning and management policies and programs.
43. By addressing these challenges and promoting effective governance, it is possible to create a more stable and sustainable environment for land use in Somalia, which can support economic development, social cohesion, and environmental protection.

138. Somalia’s ability to invest in crucial sectors such as water and agriculture remains limited. With limited fiscal resources and weak institutional capacity, Somalia faces significant challenges in achieving its development goals. The country has been struggling with poverty, conflict, and climate change for decades, which have hindered its ability to invest in these sectors.

139. Despite the government’s efforts to promote sustainable development, Somalia has not made significant progress in addressing these challenges. The country has been unable to mobilize sufficient resources and implement effective policies to improve its economic and social conditions.

140. To address these challenges, it is essential for the government of Somalia to prioritize investments in crucial sectors such as water and agriculture. This will require significant financial resources and institutional capacity building. The government should also work closely with international partners and donors to access additional funding and technical assistance.

141. In addition to investing in these sectors, the government of Somalia should also focus on promoting sustainable development more broadly. This includes addressing issues such as poverty, conflict, and climate change, which have a significant impact on the country’s economic and social conditions.

142. To achieve these goals, it is essential for the government of Somalia to work closely with international partners and donors. This will require significant financial resources and technical assistance. The government should also prioritize investments in crucial sectors such as water and agriculture, which are critical to the country’s economic and social development.

143. In conclusion, Somalia faces significant challenges in achieving its development goals due to limited fiscal resources and weak institutional capacity. To address these challenges, it is essential for the government of Somalia to prioritize investments in crucial sectors such as water and agriculture and work closely with international partners and donors. This will require significant financial resources and technical assistance.

173. Vulnerability to climate change is Somalia is underpinned by various combined factors such as extreme poverty, conflict, and weak governance. The effects of climate change are exacerbated by these factors, leading to increased vulnerability in the country.

174. Climate smart agriculture, livestock and land use (including among others) is one of the key strategies for enhancing resilience to climate change. Provision of climate-resilient water resources is also crucial for sustainable development.

175. The proposed project is aligned with current Government policies and programs to foster adaptive capacity and resilience in Somalia.

176. The project is aligned with the Somali National Adaptation Programme of Action (NAPA) to Climate Change Mitigation and Adaptation, which aims to enhance the country's ability to adapt to climate change impacts.

177. The proposed project is also in line with national priorities as set out in the National Development Plan 2020-2024 in Somalia, which focuses on sustainable development and poverty reduction.

178. The beneficiaries' needs include the urgent need to improve water infrastructure (including the designs and construction of new dams), better management of water resources and avoid negative impacts of recurrent floods. Given the country's limited access to clean water, it is important that the project includes measures to ensure sustainable use of this vital resource.

179. The proposed project also aims to enhance the resilience of agricultural production systems in Somalia by promoting climate-smart practices and improving access to climate information for farmers. This will help to reduce the vulnerability of farmers to climate change impacts, such as droughts and floods.

180. The project is aligned with the National Water Resource Strategy (NWRS) 2021-2025 in Somalia, which aims to improve water management and ensure sustainable use of this vital resource.

181. The proposed project also includes measures to enhance the resilience of agricultural production systems in Somalia by promoting climate-smart practices and improving access to climate information for farmers. This will help to reduce the vulnerability of farmers to climate change impacts, such as droughts and floods.

182. The project is also aligned with the National Agency for Disaster Management (NADM) in Somalia, which aims to enhance the country's ability to respond to and recover from disasters. This will help to ensure that the project takes into account the potential impacts of climate change on disaster risk reduction and management.

## Office of the Minister

## Ref: MO/CC/W14/2024

## Date: 12-05-2024

---

**To:** The Green Climate Fund ("GCF")

**From:** Federal Republic of Somalia, Ministry of Environment & Climate Change

---

Dear Madan, Sir,

We are writing to confirm our non-objection to the project titled "Climate Resilient Agriculture in Somalia" (GCF) by Food and Agriculture Organization of the United Nations (FAO). The project is included in the funding proposal submitted on April 28, 2024.

We acknowledge that our national process for certifying non-objection to the project has been completed and is available on the GCF website. We also confirm that the content of this letter is consistent with our previous communication regarding the project.

# Livestock Value Chain: A Comprehensive Approach to Enhancing Food Security and Sustainability

## Introduction

The livestock value chain (LVCC) is a complex network of activities that encompasses all stages from production to consumption, including feeding, breeding, health management, and marketing. This document provides an overview of the LVCC, highlighting its importance in achieving food security and sustainability goals.

## Components of the Livestock Value Chain

The LVCC consists of several key components:

1. **Feed Production**: The production of feed for livestock is a critical component of the LVCC. This includes the cultivation of crops, such as cereals and legumes, as well as the processing of animal waste into fertilizers.
2. **Livestock Production**: Livestock production involves the raising of animals for meat, milk, eggs, and other products. This includes the selection of breeding stock, feeding, and health management practices.
3. **Processing and Marketing**: The processing and marketing of livestock products involve the transformation of raw materials into finished goods, such as meat, dairy products, and animal feeds. This also includes the distribution and sale of these products to consumers.
4. **Value Chain Management**: Value chain management involves the coordination and integration of all activities in the LVCC to ensure efficient and sustainable production and distribution of livestock products.

## Challenges and Opportunities

The LVCC faces several challenges, including:

1. **Climate Change**: Climate change poses a significant threat to the livestock industry, affecting feed availability, water resources, and disease prevalence.
2. **Feed Security**: Ensuring food security for livestock is critical for maintaining stable production levels and reducing the risk of price volatility.
3. **Market Access**: Improving market access for livestock products can help increase prices and improve profitability for producers.
4. **Sustainable Practices**: Promoting sustainable practices in the LVCC, such as reducing greenhouse gas emissions and improving animal welfare, is essential for achieving long-term food security and environmental sustainability goals.

## Conclusion

The livestock value chain plays a critical role in achieving food security and sustainability goals. By addressing the challenges and opportunities facing the LVCC, we can ensure that it remains a vital component of the global food system.

8. The report also highlights the importance of integrating biodiversity conservation into project planning and implementation.
9. The report concludes that while the project has made progress in improving access to basic services, it is important to continue monitoring its impact on biodiversity and taking steps to mitigate any negative effects.

# Response from the accredited entity to the independent Technical Advisory Panel's assessment (FP24G)

## Proposal name: Climate Resilient Agriculture in Somalia (Uganda)

## Accredited entity: Food and Agriculture Organization of the United Nations

## Country: Somalia

## Project/programme size: Medium

### Impact potential

The AI acknowledges that the overall impact potential is assessed as medium to high by ITAP.

### Paradigm shift potential

The AI acknowledges that the overall paradigm shift potential is assessed as medium to high by ITAP.

### Sustainable development potential

The AI acknowledges that the overall sustainable development potential is assessed as high by ITAP.

### Needs of the recipient

The AI acknowledges that the overall needs of the recipient is assessed as high by ITAP.

### Country ownership

The AI would like to reconfirm that the Government of Somalia including the governments of federal member states in which this project operates has been extensively involved in all aspects of project's design and will be actively involved in all aspects of project's life cycle to ensure its success.

### Efficiency and effectiveness

The AI would like to reconfirm that the capacity building activities included in the first two components are designed to foster the emergence of an enabling environment that will create a sustainable climate change adaptation strategy for Somalia. The AI also acknowledges that the project strategies to overcome the barriers to adaptation and resilience to climate change have been developed.

The AI would like to also highlight that the project will generate multiple non-monetary benefits to communities currently living in extreme climate and social vulnerability.
